# The exact threshold survivor also passes the complete scalar channel

Status: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Max4 round38, the last
analytical round in the allocation with completed baseline36 and
completed ceiling38. This is not a fact and has not been submitted to
the verifier. The max2-only formal allocation is not used.

Accepted inputs are04219554e41d9750, Section10, for the original full
primitive identity, and1252b8823bb5a290, paragraphs1--2, for interpolation.
All additional arguments appear in this joint text. The reproduced
round37 proof in Part II is proof text within this same unverified
draft, not an accepted predecessor. Its historical statements that the
scalar comparison was undecided are superseded by Part I below.

No numerical evaluation, solver, machine algebra, replay, installation,
consult, subagent or formal-verifier call was used. All estimates below
are symbolic inequalities, including the rational constants.

## Result and exact scope

Let

 C=193/25, lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/1000,
 t0=(C-lambda)/(C+lambda), N0=C*t0,
 a=3/4, b=N0-15/4+epsilon,
 c=lambda*t0-epsilon, d=sqrt(2)-1,
 h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d).                                  (I.1)

The physical word has sum s=168/25 and capacity1+s=C=193/25.
The following three assertions are established together in this draft:

1. This word satisfies the precise published necessity inventory N_pub
   in Part II, Section1: cuts, clipping/returns, every retained triangle,
   all C82 values, all C83/C86 curvatures, all true scalar quadratics,
   FULL2948, the old quantitative cut-slack/ALL-prefix conditions, and
   all retained original-row calibrations, caps, mixtures and Psi on
   their stated domains. Part II contains the complete inventory,
   domain reduction, provenance, necessity proofs and exact checks.
2. The SAME word satisfies the separate COMPLETE value-optimized
   scalar channel: for every curvature theta in[0,1], the primitive
   energy is nonnegative for EVERY smoothly convexly interpolable
   choice of values on the scalar polynomial positions and gradients.
   Its exact minimum is strictly positive for0<theta<=1, and zero at0.
   Part I proves this, including the actual static attaining function
   and the precise scope of its minimizer.
3. The SAME word has an explicit static two-dimensional convex
   1-smooth function and its original four-step GD trajectory with
   primitive energy strictly below-29/20. All finite and optimum rows
   hold. Thus there is no original twenty-sign, exact-flow, full-R5
   primitive certificate for this word. Part I reproduces this witness.

Consequently N_pub AND the COMPLETE scalar channel are nonempty at the
ACTUAL threshold, but are insufficient for original-array feasibility.
The explicit nested-cut condition excludes a concrete member of that
stronger conjunction. This is not an unknown-independence inequality.

There is no assertion about all nonlinear one-dimensional functions:
the scalar channel here has the explicitly prescribed proportional
gradients below. There is also no threshold seed, global four-step
nonexistence theorem, normalized objective comparison, or solution of
the original asymptotic exact-profile Goal.

A scoped byproduct is proved in Section5a: on the physical box
h_box=(7/4,w,z,v),5/4<=w<=4/3,9/4<=z<=16/7,7/5<=v<=10/7,
the COMPLETE scalar channel is equivalent to its single unit-curvature
quadratic test. This box contains the exact threshold survivor, so
its intersection with the old system is explicitly nonempty. No claim
outside this particular box is made.

Section6a also proves that the SAME nested static function has V_h<-3/2
throughout this WHOLE physical box. Thus its original-array feasible
subset is empty, in particular on its actual C>=193/25 portion.
The expanded conjunction including the nested-cut condition is empty
there; its domain outside this box remains open.

Local notation in Part I: u,w,z,v are the four physical steps; U,W,Zc,D
are scalar coefficients defined below; A,B,Z,V are positive scalar step
factors only in Section5; P there means their product. They do not
redefine project symbols with the same printed names. Part II restarts
its own local equation labels and notation.

# Part I. Complete scalar comparison and explicit separating function

## 1. Exact bounds for the physical word

Put N=3+a+b=N0+epsilon and t=c/lambda. Since
N0+lambda*t0=C-lambda and d=lambda-2, direct substitution gives
C=5+a+b+c+d, proving the sum and capacity separately.

The elementary square comparisons give7/5<sqrt(2)<10/7 and hence
12/5<lambda<17/7. The function C(C-y)/(C+y) decreases for y>0.
Its endpoint values give

 4<178718/44400<N0<25669/6325<65/16,
 4<N<407/100, 1/4<b<8/25<1/3.                         (I.2)

The function y(C-y)/(C+y) increases for12/5<=y<=17/7, because
its derivative has positive numerator C^2-2Cy-y^2. Indeed this
numerator is greater than the value25/2 at C=15/2,y=5/2: it
increases in C when C>y and decreases in y when y>0.
At12/5 its value is1596/1265, whose excess over5/4 is59/5060,
larger than epsilon. At17/7 its value is less than9/7, since
8C<442/7. Therefore

 5/4<c<9/7, 0<d<3/7, bc<72/175<1/2.                 (I.3)

In physical notation this gives

 u=7/4, 5/4<w=1+b<4/3,
 9/4<z=1+c<16/7, 7/5<v=sqrt(2)<10/7,
 w<v<u<z, s<7, 7<C<8.                               (I.4)

The exact third-cut gap is

 N-Ct=epsilon*(1+C/lambda)>0.

Moreover

 abN<(3/4)(8/25)(407/100)=1221/1250<1.

Since d=1/lambda, these yield the strict unit-curvature inequality

 P1=abcd>0, C*P1=ab*Ct<abN<1.                       (I.5)

The letters P1 and P below are scalar products, never a certificate
matrix or a choice of original multipliers.

## 2. The complete scalar channel and its static attainment

For a fixed theta in[0,1], set

 p_0=1, p_i=product_(r<i)(1-theta*h_r),
 x_i=p_i, g_i=theta*p_i, f_i^0=theta*p_i^2/2,
 Utheta=theta*(1-theta)/2,                            (I.6)

where i=0,...,4. These positions and gradients follow ALL four
original GD updates. The values remain free subject to every finite
ordered smooth-convex interpolation inequality

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.

For f_i=f_i^0+r_i, direct expansion gives

 Q_ij=r_i-r_j-Utheta*(p_i-p_j)^2.

Let d_i be the shortest sum of squared edge lengths from p4 to p_i,
with vertices all five sampled scalar coordinates. It equals the sum
of squares of the consecutive distinct sorted gaps between these two
coordinates. To prove this, an edge spanning several sorted gaps has
squared length at least the sum of their squares, every path must
cross each intervening gap, and the sorted monotone path attains that
sum. Repeated coordinates contribute zero gaps and cause no exception.

Following the interpolation constraints along a path from4 gives
r_i-r_4>=-Utheta*d_i. These bounds are attained simultaneously by
r_i=-Utheta*d_i: the distance inequality
d_j<=d_i+(p_i-p_j)^2 gives all Q_ij<=0.

For any actual length-four trajectory the primitive energy is

 V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2
                                                -sC*g4^2.

For the base values define

 B_theta=theta*sum_(i<4)h_i[1+theta(1-h_i)]p_i^2
                            -s*theta*(1+C*theta)*p4^2.

Since every h_i is nonnegative, the exact minimum over ALL legal
scalar values is

 J_theta=B_theta-theta*(1-theta)*sum_(i<4)h_i*d_i.     (I.7)

In particular the COMPLETE scalar predicate S_scalar is J_theta>=0
for every theta in[0,1]. It is not merely B_theta>=0 and is not
silently identified with any finite list of cuts or repaired quadratics.
Multiplying all positions and gradients by any real amplitude multiplies
every path cost and this exact minimum by its square. Zero amplitude
has zero energy. Thus the normalization p_0=1 omits no amplitude or
sign case in this channel.

The minimizing values in(I.7) come from ONE STATIC function with a
nonempty minimizer set. Here is a proof that does not prescribe the
base origin as a minimizer. Put

 f_i=f_i^0-Utheta*d_i, cjet_i=g_i*x_i-f_i,
 H_i(y)=cjet_i+x_i*(y-g_i)+(y-g_i)^2/2,
 H(y)=max_(0<=i<=4)H_i(y), F(x)=sup_y[x*y-H(y)].       (I.8)

The function H-y^2/2 is convex and piecewise affine. Thus H is finite,
1-strongly convex and has quadratic growth; the supremum defining F
is finite and has a unique maximizer y(x). Strong subgradient
monotonicity gives |y(x)-y(x')|<=|x-x'|. The maximizing inequalities
give

 0<=F(x')-F(x)-y(x)(x'-x)<=(y(x')-y(x))(x'-x),

so F is differentiable with derivative y(x), is1-smooth, and is convex.
The identity H_j(g_i)-cjet_i=Q_ij<=0, with equality at j=i, shows
H(g_i)=cjet_i and x_i in partial H(g_i). Hence F interpolates every
specified position, gradient and optimized value.

Writing A_i=H_i(0), the exact minimizer set is

 argmin F=conv{(1-theta)*p_i:A_i=max_j A_j}.           (I.9)

Indeed H_i'(0)=x_i-g_i=(1-theta)p_i, and the subgradient of a finite
maximum at0 is the convex hull of the derivatives of its active terms.
The condition y(x)=0 is exactly x in partial H(0). This hull is
nonempty. Thus the minimizing scalar values are attained by a legal
static function with an actual minimum, without imposing an unsupported
optimum jet at0. Translation and a common value shift leave V_h
unchanged. A normalized objective payoff would additionally require
the true initial distance to this hull; no such payoff is asserted.

We next prove J_theta>0 for every0<theta<=1. The estimates use actual
interpolation paths, so they apply to the complete optimized channel
regardless of any changes in coordinate order.

## 3. All low curvatures:0<theta<=1/u

For a prefix of length m, let S_m=sum_(i<m)h_i and use the
chronological routes m->m-1->...->i. Their costs are
d_i^chron=sum_(j=i)^(m-1)(p_j-p_(j+1))^2.
Let E_m be its base primitive quadratic energy minus the price
theta*(1-theta)*sum_(i<m)h_i*d_i^chron. At m=4 every shortest
distance is no larger than this route cost, so J_theta>=E_4.
No assumption of monotone positions is made for this comparison.

The exact formula is

 E_m=1-(1+S_m*theta)^2*p_m^2
       -2theta^2*(1-theta)*sum_(j<m)S_(j+1)*h_j*p_j^2. (I.10)

To verify it, use

 p_j^2-p_(j+1)^2+(p_j-p_(j+1))^2=2theta*h_j*p_j^2,

then multiply the sum from j=i to m-1 by h_i and interchange finite
sums. This gives
sum_(i<m)h_i[p_i^2-p_m^2+d_i^chron]
=2theta*sum_(j<m)S_(j+1)*h_j*p_j^2.
Also the base energy can be written
1-[1+S_m*theta*(1+(1+S_m)*theta)]p_m^2
-theta*(1-theta)*sum_(i<m)h_i*p_i^2.
Substitution proves(I.10).

Append a step t to a prefix of sum S whose terminal coordinate is y.
Then the terminal coordinate becomes(1-t*theta)y and

 E_new-E_old
 =theta^3*t*(S+t)*y^2[2(S+1)-theta*t*(S+t)].          (I.11)

For a coefficient check, the new terminal factor is
(1+(S+t)theta)(1-t theta)=1+S theta-t(S+t)theta^2;
the added summand in(I.10) is(S+t)t*y^2. Expanding their difference
gives(I.11), with no restriction on the sign of1-t*theta.

Every bracket in(I.11) is positive for the current word and
0<theta<=1/u=4/7. At the first step it is2-u*theta>=1.
For the second and fourth steps, theta*t<1 and t<S+2, so
2(S+1)-theta*t*(S+t)>S+2-t>0.
At the third step S=u+w>3 and t=z<16/7. Since
theta*z<64/49<2, the bracket is bounded below by

 8-(4/7)(16/7)(3+16/7)=376/343>0.                    (I.12)

Starting from E_0=0, the first increment is
u^2*theta^3*(2-u*theta)>0 and all later increments are nonnegative.
Consequently

 J_theta>=u^2*theta^3*(2-u*theta)>0,
                                  0<theta<=1/u.     (I.13)

This includes the reversal at the long third step; chronological
routes are valid comparison paths even when they are not shortest.

## 4. All intermediate curvatures:1/u<=theta<=1/w

Use the direct routes4->i. Since d_i<=(p_i-p4)^2, formula(I.7)
implies J_theta>=E_direct, where telescoping
theta*sum_(i<4)h_i*p_i=1-p4 gives

 E_direct/theta^2
 =sum_(i<4)h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2
   +[2(1-theta)/theta^2]*p4*(1-p4)
   -s*(2/theta+s)*p4^2.                              (I.14)

On this interval put m=(u*theta-1)(1-w*theta)>=0. Then p2=-m.
Completion of the square and(I.4) give

 0<=m<=(u-w)^2/(4uw)<=1/35.                          (I.15)

For the last comparison, u-w<1/2 and4uw>35/4. Moreover
0<z*theta-1<=z/w-1<29/35<1 and |1-v*theta|<1,
because0<v*theta<=v/w<8/7<2. Thus

 |p2|,|p3|,|p4|<=1/35.

The only negative coefficient among h_i(2-h_i) is the third one,
and z(z-2)<32/49<1. Also u(2-u)=7/16. Drop the other nonnegative
coefficients in(I.14). The function(1-theta)/theta^2 decreases on
(0,1], so2(1-theta)/theta^2<=21/8. Finally
s(2/theta+s)<7(7/2+7)=147/2. Using
|p4(1-p4)|<=36/1225, we obtain

 E_direct/theta^2
 >=7/16-[1+(21/8)*36+147/2]/1225
 =7/16-169/1225>1/4.                                 (I.16)

The last inequality is equivalent to3675>2704. Therefore

 J_theta>theta^2/4>0, 1/u<=theta<=1/w.                (I.17)

This covers both signs of the final product and the zero products
at the ends; no sorting assertion is required.

## 5. All high curvatures:1/w<theta<=1

All four scalar step factors are negative. Define positive quantities

 A=u*theta-1, B=w*theta-1,
 Z=z*theta-1, V=v*theta-1, P=ABZV=p4.

Thus p1=-A,p2=AB,p3=-ABZ. Each factor increases with theta.
By(I.5),

 0<P<=P1=abcd<1/C<1/7.                               (I.18)

In(I.14) the term involving p4(1-p4) is nonnegative. Define

 U=u(2-u)=7/16, W=w(2-w)=1-b^2>8/9,
 Zc=z(z-2)=c^2-1<32/49<1, D=v(2-v)>0.

Dropping that nonnegative term and dividing by P^2 yields

 E_direct/theta^2>=P^2*L(theta),

 L(theta)=U/P^2+[W/B^2-Zc]/(Z^2*V^2)
                         +D/V^2-s^2-2s/theta.        (I.19)

We prove L is strictly decreasing on(1/w,1]. The numerator
W/B^2-Zc is positive: B<=b<1/3 gives W/B^2>8, while Zc<1.
It decreases with theta and its positive denominator Z^2*V^2
increases, so this whole ratio has negative derivative. The term
D/V^2 also decreases. In the first term,

 (U/P^2)'=-2U/P^2*(u/A+w/B+z/Z+v/V)
                                    <=-2U*u/(A*P^2).

Here U>1/4, u/A>=u/a=7/3>2 and1/P^2>49, so this derivative
is strictly less than-49. The only positive derivative in(I.19)
is2s/theta^2<2s*w^2<224/9<25. Hence

 L'(theta)<-24<0.                                    (I.20)

At theta=1 the scalar value price is zero. The telescoping identity
sum_(i<4)h_i(2-h_i)p_i(1)^2=1-P1^2 gives

 L(1)=1/P1^2-C^2>0                                  (I.21)

by(I.5). Therefore L(theta)>=L(1)>0 throughout this final interval,
and

 J_theta>=theta^2*P^2*L(theta)>0,
                                    1/w<theta<=1.   (I.22)

The boundary theta=1/w was already handled in Section4 without any
division by P. At theta=0, all coordinates coincide and all gradients
vanish, so every legal value is equal and J_0=0. Sections3--5 and
this boundary complete the proof of the FULL S_scalar predicate.

## 5a. A whole physical box where the complete scalar test reduces to theta=1

Consider any word

 h_box=(7/4,w,z,v),
 5/4<=w<=4/3, 9/4<=z<=16/7, 7/5<=v<=10/7,
 s_box=7/4+w+z+v, C_box=1+s_box,
 P_box=(3/4)(w-1)(z-1)(v-1).                         (I.22a)

Define J_theta for this word by the exact attained formula(I.7).
Then

 [J_theta>=0 for EVERY theta in[0,1]]
                    iff C_box*P_box<=1.              (I.22b)

Necessity follows at theta=1: the value price is zero and direct
telescoping gives J_1=1-C_box^2*P_box^2.

For sufficiency note first that

 133/20<=s_box<=571/84<7, C_box>7,
 w<v<7/4<z, u(2-u)=7/16,
 w(2-w)>=8/9, 0<z(z-2)<=32/49<1, v(2-v)>0.

Apply the exact path identities(I.10)--(I.11) to this word. For
0<theta<=4/7 the first increment is u^2 theta^3(2-u theta)>0,
the second and fourth brackets are positive since theta*h_j<1
and h_j<S+2, and the third bracket is at least376/343>0 because
S=u+w>=3 and z<=16/7. Thus J_theta>0 on this whole interval.

For4/7<=theta<=1/w, completion of the square gives
|(u theta-1)(1-w theta)|<=1/35. The bounds
0<z theta-1<=29/35<1 and |1-v theta|<1 imply
|p2|,|p3|,|p4|<=1/35. Substituting the coefficient bounds above
into the exact direct-route formula(I.14) gives
J_theta/theta^2>=7/16-169/1225>1/4.

For1/w<theta<=1, all A,B,Z,V in Section5 are positive. The
assumed unit condition gives0<P<=P_box<=1/C_box<1/7.
In(I.19), W/B^2>=8 and Zc<=32/49, so its middle ratio has
positive decreasing numerator and positive increasing denominator.
The first derivative is less than-49, the D/V^2 derivative is
negative, and2s_box/theta^2<224/9<25. Thus L'<-24 and
L(1)=1/P_box^2-C_box^2>=0. This proves J_theta>0 for theta<1
on this last interval, and J_1>=0 at its endpoint. Finally J_0=0.
This proves both directions of(I.22b), including equality in the
unit condition and every closed parameter boundary of(I.22a).

This is a dominance statement only on(I.22a). In that box a failed
optimized scalar test must already fail the old theta=1 quadratic;
outside the box the proof makes no such comparison. The exact word
(I.1) lies strictly inside the variable-step intervals and has the
strict unit condition(I.5), so it supplies the claimed actual-threshold
member of this comparison domain.

## 6. The same word is excluded by a static two-dimensional function

To keep the separation proof complete in Part I, we now reproduce
the actual function rather than merely referring to an unverified
earlier obstruction. Define

 t=c/(1+v)=c/lambda, q=a/(2+b),
 rho=(1+t)/(q*(1-t*q)).                              (I.23)

The bounds(I.2)--(I.4) give0<t<15/28,0<q<3/8 and tq<1.
For indices0 through4 put

 Xbase=(N,2+b,1,-c,-t), Gbase=(1,1,1,-t,-t),
 Xaux=(1,-a,-q,cq,tq), Gaux=(1,-q,-q,tq,tq),
 X_i=(Xbase_i,sqrt(rho)*Xaux_i),
 G_i=(Gbase_i,sqrt(rho)*Gaux_i),
 F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                          (I.24)

Both scalar coordinate arrays satisfy every original GD update:
a=(2+b)q and c=(1+v)t verify the two nontrivial transitions.
For saturated values f=xg-g^2/2, the residual is
Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)(x_j-g_j). The base target normals at0,1,2,3,4 are
N-1,1+b,0,-vt,0. All its rows are nonpositive. The auxiliary
normals are0,-(1+b)q,0,vtq,0. Its rows at target1 are all
nonpositive, and at target3 the only positive source row is03:

 Qaux_03=(1-tq)*vtq, Qbase_03=-(1+t)*vt.

Their combined value is zero by(I.23). Every other finite row is
nonpositive coordinatewise. This checks all twenty finite directions.
At the optimum sample(X_star,G_star,F_star)=(0,0,0), all incoming
rows Q_(i,star) are zero. The outgoing row Q_(star,i) is the
negative inner product of the gradient and its target normal.
Those products are nonnegative at every target: in the auxiliary
coordinate the only nonzero ones are(1+b)q^2 and vt^2q^2; the base
products at0,1,3 are N-1,1+b,vt^2. Thus all optimum rows hold too.

One explicit static interpolating function is

 K=conv{G0,G1,G3},
 F(X)=max_(G in K)[<X,G>-||G||^2/2].                 (I.25)

Here G2=G1 and G4=G3=-tG1, so0 belongs to K. The finite row checks
are exactly the projection normal inequalities
<G-G_i,X_i-G_i><=0 for every vertex G and therefore every G in K.
Completing the square shows that the unique maximizing gradient is
the projection of X onto K; at X_i it equals G_i and the value is F_i.
Adding the two projection normal inequalities gives the1-Lipschitz
bound for this gradient. The maximizing inequalities prove that F
is differentiable with this gradient, and F is convex as a supremum
of affine functions. Hence it is a globally defined convex1-smooth
function. Since0 belongs to K, F>=0=F(0), so it has an actual minimum.
No unique minimizer or normalized objective payoff is presumed.

The saturated values telescope exactly:

 V_h=||X0||^2-||X4+sG4||^2
    =N^2-C^2t^2+rho*(1-C^2t^2q^2),                 (I.26)

because X4=G4. For an expansion,2sum h_i<X_i,G_i>
-sum h_i^2||G_i||^2=||X0||^2-||X4||^2; the remaining terms
are-2s<X4,G4>-s^2||G4||^2.

The bounds are strict and rational. Namely
Ct=N0-(C/lambda)epsilon>4-1/300>39/10 and q>9/28, so
Ctq>351/280>5/4. Also rho>1/q>8/3. Meanwhile

 0<N^2-C^2t^2=epsilon*(1+C/lambda)*(N+Ct)
                                          <13/300<1/20.

Here C/lambda<10/3 and N+Ct<10. Substituting in(I.26) gives

 V_h<1/20+(8/3)*(-9/16)=-29/20<0.                  (I.27)

The accepted full identity04219554e41d9750, Section10, is
V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(G0,...,G4)>.
All Lambda_ij>=0 and Q_ij<=0 would make its left side negative,
while full-R5 positive semidefiniteness makes its right side
nonnegative. Thus no original full primitive array exists for(I.1).
This argument imposes no sparsity, rank, ordering or reversal assumption.

For general positive excesses with tq<1 the same construction yields
the necessary nested-cut inequality

 q(1-tq)(N^2-C^2t^2)+(1+t)(1-C^2t^2q^2)>=0.         (I.28)

The exact word(I.1) violates it while satisfying N_pub AND S_scalar.
The witness therefore establishes independence from that ACTUAL
threshold conjunction, including its optimized scalar continuum.

## 6a. The nested witness excludes the entire physical box

Take ANY word h_box in(I.22a), without assuming its unit-curvature

 a=3/4, b=w-1, c=z-1, d=v-1,
 N=3+a+b=11/4+w, t=c/(1+v), q=a/(2+b).

The following bounds hold over its closed parameter intervals:

 N<=49/12, C_box>=153/20,
 35/68<=t<=15/28, 9/28<=q<=1/3,
 tq<=5/28<1, C_box*t>=63/16,
 C_box*t*q>=81/64.                                  (I.28a)

For the two nontrivial products, multiply
(153/20)(35/68)=63/16 and(63/16)(9/28)=81/64.
These use independent valid lower bounds, not an assumption that
their endpoints can be attained simultaneously.

Therefore the positive coefficient in(I.23) satisfies

 rho=(1+t)/(q*(1-tq))>(1+t)/q>=309/68>9/2,
 1-C_box^2*t^2*q^2<=-2465/4096<-3/5.                (I.28b)

The last strict comparison is12325>12288. The base term satisfies

 N^2-C_box^2*t^2
 <=(49/12)^2-(63/16)^2=2695/2304<6/5,               (I.28c)

where the final comparison is13475<13824.

All constructions and directed-row checks(I.23)--(I.26) apply to

 V_(h_box)<6/5-(9/2)(3/5)=-3/2<0.                   (I.28d)

Thus EVERY word in(I.22a) fails the nested-cut condition(I.28),
and no such word has an original twenty-sign/full-R5 primitive
certificate. In particular the expanded threshold conjunction has
empty intersection with this box. This is a scoped exclusion, not

The exact threshold word(I.1) simultaneously proves that the old

## 1. Original problem and precise comparison inventory

For h=(h0,h1,h2,h3)>=0 put s=sum h and C=1+s. With original indices0,...,4,
write e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T, T_ii=1,T_it=h_t for t<i and0 for t>i,

omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T, and
Dprim=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)).
The sought seed has all20 off-diagonal Lambda_ij>=0, diagonal0,
L^T e=omega and M=Dprim+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 PSD on ALL of R5.

For actual GD data x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i define

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                      -sC||g4||^2.

Accepted04219554e41d9750 gives
V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g0,...,g4)>.
Actual smooth convex data obey Q_ij<=0. A negative actual V_h therefore
rules out EVERY original array, independently of its rank or support.

The published remaining-domain reduction is

 193/25<=C<87/10, 0<a,d<77/97,
 0<b,c<1552559/1081453, bc<1.

The reduction of nonnegative words, including zero and small steps, is

We do NOT claim global emptiness on this or any unexamined domain.
The constructed word will satisfy these inequalities directly.

**N1: all four cuts, all quadratics, and the scalar returns.** For j=0,...,3,

 C(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+Pj)(1+Rj).

For k in(0,1] set p_i(k)=product_(r<i)(1-k h_r),p_0=1 and require

 B_full(k)=k sum_(i<4)h_i[1+k(1-h_i)]p_i(k)^2

**N2: all adjacent triangles and their retained lifts.** Define

 kfirst=max((1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,0), Rfirst=P^2-kfirst,

To check ALL C82 parameters, set ell=ell82 and K=C^2(bc/lambda)^2.
We have

 ell>[(5/4)(7/5)(19/10)]/6=133/240>1/2,
 K<(bN)^2<16/9.                                     (16)

Here Dtail-bc>12/5-1/2=19/10 and lambda^2<6.
Any legal parameter has u>=R82>=1/a=4/3. Since b<1,
E82(u,z)>=16/9-K>0 already proves the unpriced family.
For the STRONGER all-value family use its price at most H82(u)-z^2.
Its energy is therefore at least

 (1-a^2)u^2-2(1+a)a*u+1+2(1+a)
                         +[2(1+a)+1-b^2]z^2-K.

The quadratic in u has unrestricted minimum9/16 when a=3/4.
The coefficient of z^2 is9/2-b^2>4. Thus EVERY legal parameter,
including the value-price switching branch, has energy

 >=9/16+4ell-K>9/16+2-16/9=113/144>0.                (17)

No optimized triangle formula or numerical root is required for this
check. It includes C82, C84 and the later optimized-value improvement.

For FULL2948, u0=7/4,delta0=17/16 and
nu<(1+1/13)*4/3=56/39<3/2. Products(9) give Krow=u0.
By(12), Erow>4>nu*delta0. Therefore
u0 Erow/nu>u0 delta0, proving(3) even before its nonnegative square-root
term is added. Since0<delta0<u0^2, this also proves the strict corollary
on its actual domain. No weak replacement of FULL2948 was used.

## 4. EVERY curvature, including the true last-step quadratic

The proof in Appendix B establishes the following exact sign statement:
for a word with1<h0,h3<9/5,1<h1,h2<49/20,C<87/10,bc<2+d,
the auxiliary energy for either middle cut is nonnegative at EVERY
k in(0,1] if its value at k=1 is nonnegative. It explicitly covers all
mixed signs and both middle-step orders; it is not a monotonicity claim
across a reciprocal root. Its mixed-interval estimates and high-interval
decreasing normalized energy are included in full.

Our word is in that domain. At j=1 its k=1 auxiliary energy is
1-C^2 a^2 eta^2>1-(6/13)^2>0. At j=2 it is
1-C^2 a^2 b^2 t^2>1-(abN)^2>0. Thus every middle auxiliary energy
is positive, and every expression(2) is nonnegative because V0j>=0.
This proves the WHOLE C83/C86 middle families, not just k=1.
For j=0 the affine Qcut polynomial and the first cut imply positivity
on the entire interval, as proved in Appendix B.

It remains to check the Rj=0 last cut, equivalently the actual
unrepriced quadratic energy B_full(k). Here is a direct relation that
also prevents confusing a clipped auxiliary with a true quadratic.
Let v=h3=sqrt2, H=h0+h1+h2,s=H+v, and p=p_3(k). Let Vaux_2(k)
be the third-cut auxiliary energy from Appendix B. Direct expansion gives

 B_full(k)-Vaux_2(k)
 =k^3 v^2 p^2 (1-k)
      [2-v+2s(1+Ck)(1+k)]/(1+kv)^2 >=0.              (18)

For a check before using v^2=2, the difference divided by k*p^2 is

 v[1+k(1-v)]-s(1+Ck)(1-kv)^2
                  +H[1+k(H+2v+1)]/(1+kv)^2.

Use H[1+k(H+2v+1)]=s(1+Ck)-v[1+k(v+1)]. Multiplying by(1+kv)^2
then gives k^2 v^2 times
2-v+kv-kv^2+s(1+Ck)(2-k^2v^2). At v^2=2 this is exactly
(1-k)[2-v+2s(1+Ck)(1+k)], proving(18), including p=0 without division.
Since Vaux_2>=0, all actual quadratics pass. At k=0 the zero function
has energy0. This finishes every curvature in N1 and N5.

## 5. The OLD cut-slack conditions really pass, including EVERY prefix

For this word we have the useful strict lower bounds

 beta1>4, beta2>1/100, beta3>10.                       (19)

For beta1, with B=Htail>9/2 and b<1/3,
B/(B+b)>9/10 and Cb/(B+b)<1. Thus
beta1>(121/16)(81/100)-1=8201/1600>4.
For beta2 use(10): its numerator is
epsilon(C+lambda)(Nlambda+Cc)>epsilon*10*18.
Its denominator(lambda+c)^2<16, so beta2>9/800>1/100.
For beta3 the prefix capacity is A=C-sqrt2>6, d<1/2,C<8,
and (1+d)^2=2. Hence beta3>(36-16)/2=10.

The terminal summand alone proves the jump/quadratic upper bound(4).
Indeed p3(1)=-abc and h3^2=2, so its squared contribution is

 (h3*p3(1))^2 beta3=a^2b^2c^2[(C-sqrt2)^2-C^2d^2].

Subtracting Qunit gives a^2b^2c^2(C-sqrt2)^2-1>0, because
abc(C-sqrt2)>(3/4)(1/4)(5/4)*6=45/32>1.
Every other summand is nonnegative.

For any nonnegative prefix of sum Pj and any ACTUAL convex trajectory,
convexity and GD give |f_i-f_j|<=Pj*Gmax^2. Consequently

 V_prefix>=-(4Pj^2+Pj)Gmax^2.                         (20)

To verify the coefficient, the value sum costs at most2Pj^2, the
gradient-diagonal sum costs at most sum h_i^2<=Pj^2, and the terminal
term costs Pj(Pj+1). These bounds also hold at Gmax=0 without division.
We show that each retained Rbd+Kbd Lbd exceeds this universal budget.

At j=0 the prefix is empty. At j=1, sqrt(beta1)>2 and
Drow4=sqrt(beta3)>3, so Ebd>12. Because dcut=b<1/3 and
2B/(B-dcut)>2, Lbd>72. Thus Rbd+Kbd Lbd>72>14=4(7/4)^2+7/4.

At j=2, sum_(i<2)Drow_i>Drow1>2, sqrt(beta2)>1/10,
and B=lambda<5/2. Therefore Ubd>4/25, eta_bd>4/25,
Ebd>4C*eta_bd>112/25 and, since dcut=c<4/3,
Lbd>168/25. Also Pj=N-1>3, h_j>2, so Kbd>17/2.
Thus

 Rbd+Kbd Lbd>1428/25>91/2>4Pj^2+Pj,

where Pj<13/4 was used for the last comparison.

At j=3, Ebd>=2sqrt(beta3)*Drow4=2beta3>20.
Since dcut=d<1/2, Lbd>80. Here5<Pj=C-1-sqrt2<6, so Kbd>10,
and the resulting budget is>800>150>4Pj^2+Pj.

The cut inequalities ensure Rbd>=0 and B-dcut>0 in every use above.
Equations(19)--(20) therefore prove ALL of(5), not only the earlier
listed prefix example. All saturated-cut implications are inapplicable,

## 6. Remaining original-row families and the exact scope of survival

The word has a=3/4>sqrt2-1,ab<1,cd<1,bc<1,a eta<1 and
v=sqrt2. The formulas in Appendix E define
beta=1+b,mu=1+t,ell=a^2b(1-bt),
Rcut=ell*N^2+mu-C^2a^2b*beta*t^2 and Dprefix=b^2N^2-P^2.
By(15), Rcut=a^2b*beta*(Jquad-C^2t^2)>0. Also

 Dprefix<16/9-121/16=-833/144,
 u0^2*delta0/a^2=833/144.                              (28)

For EVERY nonnegative price parameter k, therefore,
eps_k=k*Rcut-Dprefix>u0^2*delta0/a^2. The old two-energy
ratio tests and every capped or uncapped sharp-root ratio test pass
already because their right side is nonnegative whereas Dprefix<0.
Their zero-remainder equality restrictions do not apply.

In the old three-vector calibration, Appendix E gives
0<=sigma<=u0^2*Lk, where Lk=k*mu/(1+a^2k*mu)<=1/a^2.
In the later original02-row calibration, its displayed completion of
the square gives the same upper bound sigma<=u0^2/a^2. Thus every
retained coefficient obeys0<=sigma<=u0^2/a^2, regardless of its cap
branch. Write Q=Qunit and Qplus=Q+u0^2*delta0; both are positive.

For any A0>0 the EXACT unrestricted price supremum is

 sup_(r<A0/u0)[r*u0*delta0-r^2*Q/(A0-r*u0)]
       =(A0/u0^2)(sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q))^2.              (29)

To prove it put y=A0-r*u0>0. The expression is
A0*delta0+2A0*Q/u0^2-Qplus*y/u0^2-Q*A0^2/(u0^2*y).
The square of sqrt(Qplus*y)-A0*sqrt(Q/y) proves(29), with equality
at y=A0*sqrt(Q/Qplus). Restricting r by any original coefficient cap
cannot increase the supremum. Also
(sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q))^2<=Qplus-Q=u0^2*delta0.

Consequently(28) pays EVERY unmixed calibration, and Erow>nu*delta0
from Section3 pays every lambda_mix>=0 mixture:

 eps_k+lambda_mix*Erow
   >(u0^2/a^2+lambda_mix*nu)*delta0
   >=((sigma+lambda_mix*nu)/u0^2)
                         *(sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q))^2.

This directly proves the complete price/cap families for this word.
For the full terminal Psi test, the constants in Appendix E satisfy
k>k0>0 and Gcost>=1/(k-k0), with all other terms nonnegative.
Since Dprefix<0, eps=k*Rcut-Dprefix>k*Rcut, whence

 Rcut<eps/k<eps/(k-k0)<=Psi(sqrt(eps)).                (30)

The old terminal cut ensures that every radical in Psi is defined.
This uses the actual published Psi and its actual same-array budgets;
no covariance or original multiplier is independently selected.

Hence (6) satisfies N_pub AS DEFINED IN SECTION1, including every
retained domain, every curvature and every old quantitative prefix test.
This is a nonemptiness statement at the requested ACTUAL threshold.
It is not a theorem that the full primitive certificate cone is nonempty.

## 7. An explicit static function excludes the SAME threshold word

We now prove that the survivor has no full array. This new test has a
concrete independence witness against N_pub: the SAME exact word(6).
It is not another condition whose intersection with the old tests is
unknown. No comparison with S_scalar is inferred.

In fact the following construction works for any a,b,c,d>0. Put
v=1+d,t=c/(1+v),q=a/(2+b),N=3+a+b and C=5+a+b+c+d.
Assume tq<1 and define

 rho=(1+t)/(q(1-tq))>0.

Use the two scalar arrays, whose entries run from0 through4,

 Xbase=(N,2+b,1,-c,-t), Gbase=(1,1,1,-t,-t),
 Xaux=(1,-a,-q,cq,tq), Gaux=(1,-q,-q,tq,tq).           (21)

Both follow all four ORIGINAL GD steps. For Xaux the first two updates
use a=(2+b)q; the last two use c=(1+v)t. Assign values in each array
by f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2. Let

 X_i=(Xbase_i,sqrt(rho)*Xaux_i),
 G_i=(Gbase_i,sqrt(rho)*Gaux_i),
 F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                           (22)

For saturated values Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)(x_j-g_j).
The base array is the legal scalar third-cut function, so all its Q<=0.
For the auxiliary array the only nonzero target normals are

 y1=-(1+b)q, y3=v*t*q.

At target1 all source comparisons are nonpositive:
sources0,3,4 have gradients at least -q, and source2 equals -q.
At target3 the comparisons from sources1,2 are negative, from4 zero,
and the ONLY positive ordered residual is

 Qaux_03=(1-tq)*v*t*q.

Its base counterpart is Qbase_03=-(1+t)*v*t. Our rho makes
Qbase_03+rho*Qaux_03=0. All other combined finite rows remain
nonpositive. For the optimum sample0, every incoming residual
Q_(i,star)=0; the outgoing auxiliary residuals are
-(1+b)q^2 at1 and -v*t^2*q^2 at3, and zero elsewhere. The base
optimum rows are also nonpositive. This checks every original directed
finite and optimum interpolation pair.

For an explicit ONE STATIC function put

 Kphys=conv{G0,G1,G3},
 F(X)=max_(G in Kphys)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).               (23)

Here G2=G1 and G4=G3=-t*G1, so0 is in Kphys. The preceding comparisons
are precisely its projection normal inequalities at every X_i.
Thus its maximizing gradient at X_i is G_i and its value is F_i.
Completing the square identifies the optimizer with projection onto
Kphys. Adding two projection normal inequalities proves that this
projection is1-Lipschitz; the maximizing inequalities give
0<=F(X+Z)-F(X)-<grad F(X),Z><=||Z||^2. Consequently F is convex,
everywhere differentiable and1-smooth. Also F>=0=F(0), so it has
an actual minimizer. No claim of a unique minimizer or a normalized
W payoff is needed for the primitive-energy obstruction.

Saturated values telescope to V_h=||X0||^2-||X4+sG4||^2.
Here X4=G4, hence the exact value is

 V_h=N^2-C^2t^2+rho*(1-C^2t^2q^2).                  (24)

For the word(6), tq<1 since t<15/28 and q<3/8.
Moreover Ct=N0-(C/lambda)epsilon>4-1/300>39/10,
while q>9/28. Thus Ctq>351/280>5/4 and

 1-C^2t^2q^2<-9/16, rho>1/q>8/3.

By(10),

 0<N^2-C^2t^2=epsilon(1+C/lambda)(N+Ct)
                         <13/300<1/20.

Combining these exact rational bounds yields

                         V_h < -29/20 <0.            (25)

Accepted04219554e41d9750 now rules out all20-sign/fullR5 arrays for
this word. This is an actual function, not an infeasible formal Gram,
an independently selected covariance, or a numerical dual certificate.

The new necessary inequality supplied by(21)--(24), when tq<1, is

 q(1-tq)(N^2-C^2t^2)+(1+t)(1-C^2t^2q^2)>=0,          (26)

equivalently

 C^2t^2 <= [N^2(1-tq)+(1+t)/q]/(1+q).

If tq>=1 the auxiliary array is itself legal, and its energy
1-C^2t^2q^2 is negative; hence that domain is excluded directly.
No singular division is used at tq=1. The full old-system survivor
(6) violates(26), proving an ACTUAL threshold separation.

At epsilon=0 the old exact prefix-heredity condition already excludes
the analogous word: its two-step prefix violates N0*(3/4)<=2+b.
We deliberately do not use that rejected boundary as the survivor.
Section5 checks the strictly perturbed word against the complete OLD
quantitative version before(26) is introduced.

## 8. The distinct complete scalar channel and static attainment

For ANY h>=0 and theta in[0,1] define q_i=product_(r<i)(1-theta h_r),
scalar positions x_i=q_i, gradients g_i=theta q_i, and base values
f_i^0=theta q_i^2/2. Put Utheta=theta(1-theta)/2. Their base slacks are

 -Q_ij(f^0)=Utheta(q_i-q_j)^2.

Let d_i be the minimum SUM OF SQUARED GAP LENGTHS on a path from
q_4 to q_i through the five sampled coordinates. Equivalently, sort
the distinct coordinates and sum the squared adjacent gaps between
the two endpoints. For any edge spanning several gaps its squared
length is at least the sum of their squares; every path crosses each
intervening gap. The sorted monotone path attains this lower bound.
Ties add zero gaps and cause no exception.

Writing f_i=f_i^0+z_i, every original finite Q row is exactly
z_i-z_j<=Utheta(q_i-q_j)^2. Following paths from4 gives
z_i-z_4>=-Utheta*d_i. These simultaneous lower bounds are attained
by z_i=-Utheta*d_i, since d_j<=d_i+(q_i-q_j)^2. As every h_i>=0,
the exact scalar value minimum is therefore

 J_theta=B_full(theta)-theta(1-theta)sum_(i<4)h_i*d_i.  (27)

This proves the channel/witness identity, including theta=0,1 and
coincident samples. The COMPLETE S_scalar predicate is J_theta>=0
for EVERY theta in[0,1]. Merely passing B_full and C83 is a different
statement. The finite scalar path calculation also gives an attaining
terminal-source flow, but makes no PSD assertion about that flow.

For completeness, these optimized finite values are always attained
by one real static smooth convex function with a nonempty minimizer
set, without retaining the base origin as a prescribed minimizer.
Set f_i=f_i^0-Utheta*d_i, cjet_i=g_i x_i-f_i and

 H_i(w)=cjet_i+x_i(w-g_i)+(w-g_i)^2/2,
 H(w)=max_i H_i(w), F(x)=sup_w[xw-H(w)].

The function H is finite and1-strongly convex with quadratic growth;
the supremum has a unique maximizing w(x). Strong subgradient
monotonicity proves |w(x)-w(y)|<=|x-y|, and the two maximizing
inequalities prove differentiability with F'=w. Also
H_j(g_i)-cjet_i=Q_ij<=0, with equality at j=i. Hence
H(g_i)=cjet_i and x_i is its subgradient there, proving interpolation
of all five prescribed positions, gradients and values.

Writing A_i=H_i(0), the minimizer set is

 argmin F=conv{(1-theta)q_i:A_i=max_j A_j}.

Indeed these are precisely the subgradients of the finite maximum H
at0; the conjugate gradient equals0 exactly at such subgradients.
Thus a minimizer exists. No original optimum at0 is silently assumed,
and V_h is invariant under common function-value shifts or translations.
This establishes the exact minimizer scope of(27). For a normalized
objective payoff the true distance to this hull would still have to be
paid; no such payoff is asserted here.

The word(6) has been proved to satisfy N_pub and to fail the NEW
two-dimensional witness(26). Whether it satisfies the additional
S_scalar channel(27) remains a sharply delimited question for the
remaining authorized analytical round. A scalar violation would be
an additional legal obstruction, not a defect in its proved N_pub
survival. If the scalar channel also passes, then(26) separates even
N_pub AND S_scalar. Neither stronger conclusion is claimed now.

# Appendix A. Reproduced domain reduction, complete triangles, clipping and return proofs

The following is reproduced proof text, not an accepted predecessor. Local equation labels and local notation restart within each historical transcript. Its scope is the one stated in the main inventory; no historical allocation or handoff instruction is renewed.

## 1. Interface and the prerequisite package

Write

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), C=5+a+b+c+d,
    P=2+a, Q=3+a+b, D=2+d, H=3+c+d,
    k=max((1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,0), R=P^2-k,
    eta=b/H, J=R-k*eta.

Appendix A derives, directly from the FULL original seed interface, the
following prerequisite package for every C>=193/25:

    193/25<=C<87/10,
    0<a,d<77/97<4/5,
    0<b,c<1552559/1081453<3/2,
    Ca<=C-1-a, Cb<=PH, Cc<=QD, Cd<=C-1-d,             (1)
    Cabcd<=1, Ccd<=Q,
    ab[1+a(C+1)]<=H,
    bc[R+b(P+C)]<=D*J,
    cd[1+c(Q+C)/Q^2]<=1.                              (2)

In particular cd<1. The last inequality has a strictly positive factor
greater than one. Appendix A gives every legal cut function, all three
adjacent triangles, the backward middle lift, the mixed cut, and the
boundary arguments that force all four steps above one. It retains all
twenty original interpolation multipliers and all five formal gradients.

There is a useful exact rewriting of the already-derived mixed cut:

                       C^2*eta^2<=J.                 (3)

For a<=sqrt(2)-1, k=0 and (3) is the second cut in (1).
For a>sqrt(2)-1 put u=1+a in Appendix A equation (20). Dividing that
inequality by a gives

    (u^2+u-1)/a+u(2-u^2)*eta/a-C^2*eta^2
        =a+3+1/a-k*eta-C^2*eta^2=J-C^2*eta^2.

The domain a*eta<1 follows from eta<=P/C and a<4/5,P<14/5,C>7.
In particular J>0, because C,eta>0. Thus no denominator involving J
is being assigned a sign from an unverified numerical observation.

Appendix B derives the exact elimination of ALL lower clipping parameters
for the family f'_alpha(x)=clamp(x,[-alpha,1]), x0=P. It gives

    Cb/H<=P,
    Cbd/(2+c)<=P,
    C|bc-(1+d)min(bc,1)|<=P,
    Cd<=P if bc>=1.                                  (4)

These are necessary, not sufficient for the full seed. Their derivation
uses a single static smooth convex function for each parameter and all
four actual steps. Both terminal clipping branches are included.

## 2. The entire bc>=1 branch is impossible

First observe the uniform identity and bound

    R=(2+a)^2                     if a<=sqrt(2)-1,
    R=a+3+1/a                     if a>=sqrt(2)-1,
    0<R<=3+2sqrt(2)<6.                                  (5)

The first expression increases up to the joining point. The second has
derivative 1-1/a^2<0 for sqrt(2)-1<a<1. Both have value
3+2sqrt(2) at the joining point. The final strict bound follows by
squaring sqrt(2)<3/2.

Suppose bc>=1. The middle inequality in (2) and J<=R imply

    R+b(P+C)<=D*J<=D*R,
    b(P+C)<=(1+d)R<=(1+P/C)R,
    b<=R/C<6/C.                                          (6)

Here the second line uses Cd<=P from (4), valid on this entire branch.
Since bc>=1, (6) forces c>C/6.

On the other hand, P<14/5 and C>7 give d<=P/C<2/5. Thus D<12/5.
Since C>15/2, the ratio z=D/C is strictly less than 8/25. The third
cut in (1), using Q=C-D-c, rearranges to

    c/C<=z(1-z)/(1+z).

The derivative of z(1-z)/(1+z) has numerator 1-2z-z^2, positive
on [0,8/25]. Consequently

    c/C<136/825<1/6,
    1/6-136/825=1/550>0.                                 (7)

This contradicts c>C/6. Therefore EVERY full seed in the entire original
capacity range must satisfy

                              bc<1.                     (8)

No ordering, equality, fixed capacity, symmetry, prefix heredity, or
support restriction was used. The boundary bc=1 is excluded as well.
Only the prerequisites reproduced in Appendices A and B were needed.

## 3. A lifted return witness and its global consequences

The next result is new to this recovery round. It modifies the first
gradient of the partial-tail return witness, rather than changing its
curvature. Thus it is distinct from max3's assigned curvature family.
Throughout the compact domain, every full seed must satisfy

    C^2*b^2*d^2<=R*(2+c)^2-k*b*(2+c).                    (9)

For a<=sqrt(2)-1 this is exactly the second condition in (4).
For a>sqrt(2)-1 the new right side is strictly smaller than
P^2*(2+c)^2. The entire witness and every ordered interpolation pair
are checked next.

Put beta=b/(2+c), let u=(1,0), and suppose a>sqrt(2)-1. The compact
bounds give beta<3/4, a*beta<3/5, and d*beta<3/5. Define

    q=(1+(1-a)*beta)/a,
    z=-beta*u+sqrt(q-beta^2)*(0,1),
    Kproj=conv{u,-beta*u,z}.

The radicand is (1+beta)(1-a*beta)/a>0. The optimum gradient zero
lies in the segment joining u and -beta*u. Use the five samples

    x=(u+(1+a)*z, u, -b*u, -beta*u, d*beta*u),
    g=(z, u, -beta*u, -beta*u, d*beta*u),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,

and the zero optimum jet. All four GD identities have exactly their
original step lengths: in particular b=(2+c)*beta. Every listed g_i
belongs to Kproj, since 0<d*beta<1.

For these assigned values Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>. To check every
finite and optimum ordered pair it suffices to check the three vertex
normal inequalities at every target sample. Targets 1,3,4 have x_j=g_j,
so their normal is zero. At target 2 the normal is -(b-beta)u, with
b-beta>0. The comparison with z is zero since <z,u>=-beta; comparison
with u is negative. At target 0 the normal is u+a*z. Since

    <u,z>+a||z||^2=1-a*beta,

the comparison with any t*u, -beta<=t<=1, is exactly

    <t*u-z,u+a*z>=(1-a*beta)(t-1)<=0.

The comparison with z itself is zero. These comparisons include the
zero optimum and the terminal gradient. Hence all original ordered Q
are nonpositive. Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 realizes these complete
finite data by one global convex differentiable 1-smooth function with
a minimizer at zero. No pre-existing Gram or omitted pair is used.

The saturated-value telescoping identity from Appendix A and accepted
04219554e41d9750 gives

    V=||x0||^2-C^2*d^2*beta^2,
    ||x0||^2=1-2(1+a)*beta+(1+a)^2*q
             =P^2-k*(1+beta)=R-k*beta.

Thus V>=0 implies (9). When a<=sqrt(2)-1, the original scalar witness
has x0=P and proves the same formula with k=0. This proves (9) on
the whole compact domain, including the joining point.

Let lambda=1+sqrt(2). Equations (5) and (9) imply the uniform return
bound Cbd<=lambda*(2+c). On the remaining branch bc<1, (4) also gives
Cbcd<=P, while the full quadratic gives Cbcd<=1/a. The elementary
function min(2+a,1/a) is at most lambda: its increasing and decreasing
branches meet only at a=sqrt(2)-1. Therefore

    Cbcd<=lambda,
    Cbd<=lambda*min(2+c,1/c)<=lambda^2.                 (10)

The last inequality follows from the same increasing/decreasing
comparison in c. In addition, (3) gives

                             Cb<=lambda*H.             (11)

These are globally valid necessary bounds on the full threshold seed
domain. They are weaker than retaining (9), (3), and the actual a,c.
They do not prove that the total capacity is below 193/25.

## 1. Interface and energy

Let h=(h_0,h_1,h_2,h_3)>=0, s=sum h_i, C=1+s>=193/25.
Indices i,j range over0,...,4. Set p_i=1+sum_(t<i)h_t, so p_0=1,p_4=C.
Let e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T. Define T_ii=1,T_it=h_t for t<i,T_it=0 for t>i,
Lambda_ii=0,Lambda_ij>=0,L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
omega=(-2h_0,-2h_1,-2h_2,-2h_3,2s)^T, and
D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_3(2-h_3),-s(s+2)).
The full interface is

    L^T e=omega, M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0.                 (1)

No multiplier support, rank, remainder, symmetry, or step order is fixed.
For arbitrary formal GD data x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, define

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f_4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
      -s(s+1)||g_4||^2.

Put delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2 and y_i=x_i-g_i=x_0-(Tg)_i.
Then Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>, while
V=-omega^T delta+sum_i D_ii||g_i||^2. Value flow in(1) therefore gives

    V+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij = sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>. (2)

In particular every feasible full certificate implies V>=0 on every legal
convex differentiable1-smooth trajectory with a minimizer. This direction
requires neither certificate necessity nor any duality assertion.

## 2. Legal cut witnesses at every position

Fix j<4, put b=h_j,P=sum_(i<j)h_i,R=sum_(i>j)h_i, and first assume b>1.
Set a=(b-1)/(R+1)>0. Define the scalar function

    f(x)=x-1/2                     if x>=1,
         x^2/2                     if -a<=x<=1,
         -a*x-a^2/2                if x<=-a.

Its derivative is clamp(x,[-a,1]), which is continuous, nondecreasing,
and1-Lipschitz. The function is convex1-smooth and has a minimizer at0.
Start at x_0=P+1. Before and at j all gradients are1, and x_j=1.
After step j, x_(j+1)=1-b=-a(R+1). All subsequent gradients equal-a,
since the remaining nonnegative steps move monotonically to x_4=-a.
Every sampled value obeys f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, and x_4=g_4.

For any vector-valued data obeying these two identities, GD telescoping gives

    2sum_(i<4)h_i f_i+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
      =||x_0||^2-||x_4||^2,
    V=||x_0||^2-C^2||g_4||^2.                         (3)

Applied to this scalar function, nonnegative V implies

    C(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+P)(1+R), for every j=0,...,3.      (4)

If h_j<=1 the same inequality is automatic. Thus(4) holds on every
zero-step boundary too. Failure of any inequality is a specific legal
negative-V function, hence rules out the full interface(1).

## 3. Prefix bounds and compact capacity

For fixed C>1 define the increasing rational map

    R_C(z)=1+2Cz/(C+z), z>=0,
    r_k=R_C iterated k times at1, k=0,1,2,3.

Rearrangement of(4) gives p_(i+1)<=R_C(p_i). Conversely this inequality
is equivalent to(4) on the nonnegative schedule domain. Induction yields
p_i<=r_i. The cut constraints are invariant under reversing the four
steps, regardless of whether the original certificate admits reversal.
Applying the same induction to the reversed cut inequalities gives

    p_i >= C+1-r_(4-i).                               (5)

Only the cases i=1,2,3 are needed; r_0=1. Explicitly,

    r_1=(3C+1)/(C+1),
    r_2=(7C^2+6C+1)/(C^2+4C+1),
    r_3=(15C^3+23C^2+9C+1)/(C^3+11C^2+7C+1).          (6)

Taking i=2 in(5) and p_2<=r_2 gives C+1<=2r_2, hence

    C^3-9C^2-7C-1<=0.                                (7)

This polynomial is strictly increasing for C>=7: its derivative
3C^2-18C-7 is positive there and increasing. At C=39/4 its value is
131/64>0. Since C>=193/25>7, we conclude

    193/25 <= C < 39/4.                               (8)

In particular this argument covers all larger candidate sums; it did not
replace the target by the slice sum h=168/25.

## 4. All four steps are uniformly positive and bounded

The first and last cut inequalities give

    h_0,h_3 <= 2C/(C+1) < 78/43.                      (9)

For the lower bounds, subtract the upper prefix bound from the lower
bound for the next prefix in(5). This gives

    h_0,h_3 >= C-r_3 = N(C)/(C^3+11C^2+7C+1),
    h_1,h_2 >= C+1-r_1-r_2 = N(C)/((C+1)(C^2+4C+1)), (10)
    N(C)=C^4-4C^3-16C^2-8C-1.

The endpoint lower bound exceeds1/2 exactly when

    A(C)=2C^4-9C^3-43C^2-23C-3>0.

The middle lower bound exceeds4/5 exactly when

    B(C)=5C^4-24C^3-100C^2-60C-9>0.

At C=77/10 these polynomials respectively equal1922412/10000 and
2197285/10000. They are strictly increasing for C>=7: their derivatives
are bounded below respectively by29C^2-86C-23 and68C^2-200C-60,
both positive and increasing on that interval. Since193/25>77/10,

    h_0,h_3>1/2, h_1,h_2>4/5.                         (11)

For completeness, the middle upper bound uses the same cuts, without
assuming either middle step exceeds1. Write

    G_C(z)=R_C(z)-z=1+z(C-z)/(C+z).

For1<=z<=r_1<3 its derivative is
(C^2-2Cz-z^2)/(C+z)^2>0: its numerator is at least C^2-6C-9,
which is positive for C>=15/2. Thus h_1<=G_C(p_1)<=G_C(r_1), and
the reversed cut constraints give the same bound for h_2. Calculation yields

    G_C(r_1)=4C^3/((C+1)(C^2+4C+1)).                  (12)

The right side is strictly increasing in C>0: its logarithmic derivative
has positive numerator5C^2+10C+3 after multiplication by positive
denominators. At39/4 its value is237276/92923<13/5, with the difference
21619/464615. Consequently

    1/2<h_0,h_3<78/43, 4/5<h_1,h_2<13/5.             (13)

This excludes every zero-step boundary for this threshold by actual
witnesses. It does not assert h_i>1, ordering, or reversal of certificates.

## 5. Full multiplier compactness from the common-gradient kernel

This step bounds all20 original multipliers, including every cycle.
The vector p=Te has entries p_i. Since

    sum_i D_ii=-s^2-sum_(i<4)h_i^2,
    omega^T p=s^2+sum_(i<4)h_i^2,

equations(1) give e^T M e=0. Positive semidefiniteness implies Me=0.
For clarity this implication follows by inserting e+t v into the quadratic
form: a nonzero linear coefficient would violate nonnegativity for one
sign of sufficiently small t. Calculation of each component gives

    2De+T^T omega=-diag(p)omega,
    Lp=diag(p)omega.                                  (14)

Define A_ij=Lambda_ij p_j for i!=j and A_ii=0. Since omega_i=out_i-in_i,
equation(14) says sum_j Lambda_ij p_j=p_i in_i. Therefore

    sum_j A_ij=sum_j A_ji for every i.                 (15)

Thus A is a nonnegative circulation. Write c_i=1/p_i. Substituting
Lambda_ij=A_ij c_j into the original value flow and using(15) gives

    sum_i c_i omega_i
      =sum_(i,j) A_ij(c_i c_j-c_i^2)
      =-1/2 sum_(i,j)A_ij(c_i-c_j)^2.

Equivalently, the entire circulation has the fixed Dirichlet energy

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij (p_i-p_j)^2/(p_i^2 p_j)
       =4[sum_(i<4)h_i/p_i-s/C] <4s<35.              (16)

All steps exceed1/2 by(13). Consequently |p_i-p_j|>1/2 for i!=j.
Also every p_i<10 by(8), so p_i^2 p_j<1000. Every coefficient of
Lambda_ij on the left of(16) exceeds1/4000. Hence

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij < 140000.                      (17)

This is a bound on the complete multiplier vector, not a choice of sparse
support. No skew freedom or PSD remainder is fixed. In particular all
possible solutions of(1) at the requested threshold belong to the closed
bounded box obtained by replacing the strict inequalities in(8),(13),(17)
by non-strict ones. The equations and PSD conditions are continuous, so
their solution set in this box is compact (possibly empty).

## 6. A suffix witness and a full two-coordinate mixed witness

The first new constraint is

    C |(1-h_2)(1-h_3)| <= 1+h_0+h_1.                  (18)

To prove it, take the1-smooth convex function with derivative min(x,1),
whose values are x^2/2 for x<=1 and x-1/2 for x>=1. Start at
x_0=1+h_0+h_1. The first two gradients are1, x_2=1, x_3=1-h_2<=1,
and x_4=(1-h_2)(1-h_3). Every sample satisfies f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.
Without imposing x_4=g_4, the same telescoping calculation as(3) gives

    V=||x_0||^2-||x_4+s g_4||^2.                     (19)

If x_4<=1, then g_4=x_4 and(19) is precisely(18). If x_4>1,
g_4=1 and x_4+s>C>x_0, since h_2+h_3>0 by(13), giving V<0.
This latter case is excluded, proving(18) for every candidate.

Here is a second necessary inequality with its complete interpolation
proof. Suppose u=h_0>1, b=h_1>1, put a=u-1,A=1+u,
R=h_2+h_3,D_tail=1+R, eta=(b-1)/D_tail, and assume a*eta<1.
Then every feasible seed satisfies

    H_mix(u,C,eta)=u^2+u-1+u(2-u^2)eta
                   -(u-1)C^2 eta^2 >=0.             (20)

Take the base cut data from Section2 with j=1. Its gradients are1
at i=0,1 and-eta at i=2,3,4; its initial point is A and its final
point and gradient are-eta. Its proximal points y_i=x_i-g_i are
y_0=u,y_1=0 and y_i=-eta*sum_(t=i)^3 h_t for i>=2.
Its energy is F_base=A^2-C^2 eta^2.

Define auxiliary scalar data by x_0=g_0=1, x_1=g_1=-a,
g_i=a*eta for i>=2, and actual GD recursion for x_i. Thus
x_2=a(b-1)=a*eta*D_tail, x_4=g_4=a*eta; all earlier auxiliary
proximal points are0 and y_i=a*eta*sum_(t=i)^3 h_t for i>=2.
Assign f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, and include the zero optimum jet.

For any data with this value assignment, direct expansion gives
Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)y_j. Optimum residuals are Q_(i,star)=0 and
Q_(star,i)=-g_i y_i. All auxiliary optimum residuals are nonpositive.
The only positive auxiliary finite residuals are, for j=2,3,

    Q_aux,(0,j)=(1-a*eta)a*eta*sum_(t=j)^3 h_t.

Every other finite residual is nonpositive: target j=0,1,4 has y_j=0;
two post-cut gradients agree; the pre-cut gradient-a is smaller than
a*eta. The corresponding base residuals are

    Q_base,(0,j)=-(1+eta)eta*sum_(t=j)^3 h_t.

With epsilon=(1+eta)/(a(1-a*eta))>0, take the orthogonal sum of the
base and sqrt(epsilon) times the auxiliary positions and gradients,
and the sum of their function values with weight epsilon. All Q add;
these two possible positive residuals cancel, and every other one remains
nonpositive. The full finite array, including the optimum, is realized
by one globally convex1-smooth function by fact1252b8823bb5a290.
Its energy is

    F_base+epsilon(1-C^2 a^2 eta^2)
       = u*H_mix(u,C,eta)/(a(1-a*eta)).                (21)

This equality follows by expanding both sides and using
1-a^2 A^2=u^2(2-u^2). All denominators are positive, proving(20).
Every original directed interpolation pair is included in this argument.

## 7. Both middle steps are strictly greater than one

For this section write a=h_0-1,b=h_1-1,c=h_2-1,d=h_3-1.
The previously proved bounds imply

    a,d<9/11; b,c<14/9; a+b+c+d=C-5>=68/25.           (22)

Indeed78/43<20/11 and237276/92923<23/9; the latter difference is
1745/836307. These bounds allow negative a,b,c,d initially.

First suppose h_1<=1, so b<=0. Then

    c+d >=68/25-a-b >68/25-9/11=523/275.

As c,d<14/9 and523/275>14/9, both c and d are positive.
The identity (14/9-c)(14/9-d)>0 implies

    cd > (14/9)(523/275-14/9)=11998/22275.

But(18) requires Ccd<=3+a+b<42/11. This is impossible, since

    (193/25)(11998/22275)-42/11=189364/556875>0.

Hence h_1>1.

Next suppose h_2<=1, so c<=0. From(22),
b>=68/25-a-d>68/25-18/11>0, so the second-step cut is available.
Its inequality is

    Cb <= (2+a)(3+c+d).

If a<=3/5, its left side is at least
(193/25)(68/25-3/5-9/11), while its right side is at most
(2+3/5)(3+9/11). The first exceeds the second by844/6875>0.
Thus a>3/5 and u=h_0 lies in(8/5,20/11).

Let C_0=193/25,D_0=42/11 and

    eta=b/(3+c+d)=(C-1-u)/(3+c+d)-1,
    eta_0(u)=(C_0-1-u)/D_0-1.

The denominator3+c+d is positive and at most D_0, while C-1-u>0.
Therefore eta>=eta_0(u)>0 on this interval. The cut itself yields

    a*eta <= a(1+u)/C=(u^2-1)/C<1.

Consequently the mixed necessary inequality(20) applies. Since
u>=8/5>sqrt(2), its coefficient u(2-u^2) is negative and u-1>0.
H_mix decreases as eta>0 increases and as C>0 increases. Thus

    0 <= H_mix(u,C,eta) <= H_mix(u,C_0,eta_0(u)).       (23)

The final expression is a rational quartic in u. Put
u=8/5+(20/11-8/5)z for0<=z<=1, and define
B_(4,k)(z)=binomial(4,k)z^k(1-z)^(4-k). Direct multiplication of the
displayed definition of H_mix gives

    H_mix(u,C_0,eta_0(u))=sum_(k=0)^4 beta_k B_(4,k)(z),

where the exact coefficients in increasing k are

    -124646528/95703125,
    -3776352043/3158203125,
    -1682896352/1654296875,
    -98764097577/127380859375,
    -36497765912/76428515625.

All are strictly negative. The Bernstein basis terms are nonnegative
and sum to1; hence this quartic is strictly negative throughout the
whole interval, contradicting(23). This proves

    h_1>1 and h_2>1.                                  (24)

No conclusion h_0>1 or h_3>1 has been assumed or obtained here.
The fixed quartic expansion is small exact algebra, not a numerical
parameter scan or a solver certificate.

## 8. General cut/quadratic repair and two additional tests

The mixed construction has a useful full-pair version at either middle
cut, retaining the entire quadratic prefix. Fix j in{1,2}, let h_j>1,
P=sum_(i<j)h_i,R=sum_(i>j)h_i,eta=(h_j-1)/(1+R), and define

    z_0=1,z_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t) for1<=i<=j,
    q=-eta*z_j,
    beta=max(0,max_(0<=i<=j) q(z_i-q)).

Then another necessary inequality is

    beta*((1+P)^2-C^2 eta^2)
       +(1+eta)eta*(1-C^2 q^2) >=0.                  (25)

Here is a joint derivation, including beta=0. Use the cut function of
Section2 as the base. For auxiliary data set x_i=g_i=z_i for i<=j,
set g_i=q for i>j, and follow GD. The first post-cut point is
x_(j+1)=q(1+R), and x_4=q. All auxiliary proximal points before or
at j are0; later they equal q times the remaining step sum. Assign
f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2 and include the zero optimum. The optimum residuals
are nonpositive. A possible positive finite residual has source i<=j
and target k>j and equals q(z_i-q) times that remaining step sum.
The corresponding base residual is-(1+eta)eta times that sum.

If beta>0, multiply the auxiliary Gram/values by
epsilon=(1+eta)eta/beta and orthogonally add to the base. Every original
residual is nonpositive; fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives one legal function.
Its energy is the left side of(25) divided by beta, proving the inequality.
If beta=0 the auxiliary data alone are legal, and their energy is
1-C^2 q^2. This proves(25) in this case too, without division by zero.
No Gram from a different schedule was reused.

Full-curvature quadratic data give the additional necessary condition

    C*|product_(i=0)^3(1-h_i)| <=1.                   (26)

This is the actual function f(x)=x^2/2 started at1; equation(3) applies.

There is also a nonlinear scalar condition that is stronger than merely
imposing(26). Since both middle steps exceed1 by Section7, put
q_mid=(h_1-1)(h_2-1)>0. Then

    C*|q_mid-h_3*min(q_mid,1)| <=1+h_0.              (27)

Use again the function with derivative min(x,1), this time started at
x_0=1+h_0. Its first step reaches1; the next two points are
x_2=1-h_1 and x_3=q_mid. Thus x_4=q_mid-h_3*min(q_mid,1).
All values have the saturated form, so(19) holds. If x_4>1 then
x_4+s>C>1+h_0 and V<0. Otherwise g_4=x_4 and(19) gives(27).
The inequality in(27) itself forces |x_4|<1, because 1+h_0<C.

The cuts yield a quantitative endpoint-sum constraint as well. Put
A=1+h_0,D_end=1+h_3,E_end=A+D_end and b=h_1-1,c=h_2-1.
Rearranging the cuts at1 and2 gives

    b<=A(C-A)/(C+A), c<=D_end(C-D_end)/(C+D_end).

The function z(C-z)/(C+z) has second derivative-4C^2/(C+z)^3<0.
Adding these two inequalities and using concavity at their midpoint yields

    C-E_end-1=b+c<=E_end*(2C-E_end)/(2C+E_end),
    E_end >= 2C(C-1)/(3C+1).                         (28)

In particular at least one endpoint exceeds1. Indeed if both were at
most1 then E_end<=4, and(28) would imply C^2-7C-2<=0. That polynomial
is positive at193/25 (value2224/625) and strictly increasing thereafter.
Thus a remaining candidate has three or four steps>1; the possible step
at most1 can only be one endpoint. No endpoint has been deleted.

## 9. A sharper capacity cap from cut products and the actual quadratic

The preceding constraints improve the capacity bound further to

    193/25<=C<87/10.                                 (29)

For a contradiction suppose C>=87/10. Define the following scalar
functions (distinct from the matrix and interpolation notation):

    d_cap(C)=(C+1)(C^2+4C+1),
    r_cap(C)=(C-1)/(C+1),
    t_cap(C)=4C^3/d_cap(C)-1,
    ell_cap(C)=2C(C-1)/(3C+1)-4,
    k_cap(C)=C-5-2r_cap(C).

Write a=h_0-1,b=h_1-1,c=h_2-1,d=h_3-1 as before. Equations(9),(12),(28)
give a,d<=r_cap, b,c<=t_cap, a+d>=ell_cap, and b+c>=k_cap.
Multiplication of the nonnegative factors r_cap-a,r_cap-d and
t_cap-b,t_cap-c yields

    ad>=r_cap*(ell_cap-r_cap),
    bc>=t_cap*(k_cap-t_cap).                         (30)

All four factors on the right are positive and increasing for C>=87/10.
Here are detailed sign bounds. Clearly r_cap>0,r_cap'>0 there, and
t_cap'>0 follows from

    t_cap'=4C^2(5C^2+10C+3)/d_cap(C)^2.

For C>=8, d_cap(C)>C^3 gives t_cap'<403/1024, while
k_cap'=1-4/(C+1)^2>=77/81; hence k_cap-t_cap is strictly increasing.
Also

    ell_cap'=(6C^2+4C-2)/(3C+1)^2>384/625,
    r_cap'=2/(C+1)^2<=2/81

for C>=8, proving ell_cap-r_cap is strictly increasing. The first lower
bound uses3C+1<=(25/8)C and6C^2+4C-2>6C^2. At C=87/10 the exact
values of r_cap,t_cap,ell_cap-r_cap,k_cap-t_cap are respectively

    77/97,
    1552559/1081453,
    19728/131435,
    7318711/10814530,

which are all positive. Positivity in(30) also forces a,d,b,c positive:
their pairwise sums are positive, and their pairwise products are positive.
Consequently the full-curvature constraint(26) requires C*ad*bc<=1.
But the increasing lower bound from(30), evaluated at87/10, exceeds1 by

    9782467331765167/1375525927835685125>0.

This proves(29) for every larger sum, without assuming saturation in(26).

The monotonic formulas(9),(12) now refine the step upper bounds to

    1/2<h_0,h_3<174/97,
    1<h_1,h_2<2634012/1081453.                        (31)

Finally p_i<9 and4s<154/5 in the exact circulation identity(16). Its
coefficients still exceed1/(4*9^3)=1/2916, so

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij<449064/5<90000.               (32)

Thus(29),(31),(32) supersede the earlier coarse compact box; the sharper
pointwise rational cut bounds remain available inside that box.

## 11. Adjacent triangle witness, rederived in this joint argument

The shared UNVERIFIED C80 finding7b447329aac48edd suggested this construction.
It is awareness only: the geometry, every interpolation normal, and the
energy identity needed here are rederived in this section. The only fact
used for realization is1252b8823bb5a290.

Write h=(a,b,c,d), where b,c>1 and a,d>=0. Define

    P=a+1, B=b-1, E=c-1, D=d+1,
    U=b+c+d, C=P+U.

First suppose D>B*E and set

    Kappa=B^2*E+U*(D-B*E), t=B^2*E/Kappa,
    u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))), v=-(B*u+D*w)/c.

Kappa>0 and0<t<1. The convex hull of u,v,w contains0, since
B*u+c*v+D*w=0 with all three coefficients positive. Set

    x=(P*u,u,-B*u,D*w,w), g=(u,u,v,w,w),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,

and include the zero optimum jet. The four GD identities follow from
c*v=-B*u-D*w and D=d+1. Direct inner products give

    ||u||^2=1, <u,w>=||w||^2=t,
    <u,v>=-(B+D*t)/c, <v,w>=-(B+D)*t/c.

All sampled interpolation inequalities follow by checking that x_i-g_i
is an outward normal to this triangle at g_i. At i=0 that normal is
a*u, with nonpositive pairing with v-u and w-u. At i=1,4 it is zero.
At i=2 put y=-B*u-v. Expanding the displayed inner products gives

    <w-v,y>=(-B^2*E+Kappa*t)/c^2=0,
    <u-v,y>=-B*(1-t)*E/c<0.

For the first expansion, the coefficient of t is
D(2B+D)+c(D-BD+B)-B*c^2, which equals Kappa after substituting
E=c-1,U=B+c+D. For the second expansion the difference between the
two sides, multiplied by c^2, is-B^2*E+Kappa*t=0. At i=3 the normal
d*w pairs with u-w to zero and with v-w to
-d*t*(1+(B+D)/c)<=0. This exhausts the vertex comparisons. As0 is in
their convex hull, it also exhausts the optimum comparisons.

Indeed for these assigned values Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>, so all the
original ordered Q are nonpositive, Q_(i,star)=0, and Q_(star,i)<=0.
Fact1252b8823bb5a290 realizes one global convex1-smooth function with
a minimizer at0 and precisely these GD data. The terminal x_4=g_4=w,
so(3) gives V=P^2-C^2*t. A direct expansion gives

    C^2 B^2 E-P^2 Kappa
       =U*{B*E[P^2+B(P+C)]-P^2 D}.

Consequently feasible full certificates must satisfy

    B*E*[1+B(P+C)/P^2] <= D.                         (34)

This condition applies without imposing D>B*E in advance. If instead
D<=B*E, the legal function with derivative min(x,1), started at P,
has x=(P,1,-B,B*E,B*E-D+1) and g=(1,1,-B,1,1). Its final point
is at least1, and(19) gives

    V=P^2-[P+(B+1)(E+1)]^2<0.

Thus the complementary regime is impossible, proving(34) for every
candidate with b,c>1. The inequality itself implies D>B*E. No previous
word's Gram, an assumed primitive subword, or a numerical proposal has
been adopted in this argument.

The same construction proves(34) at any adjacent pair of steps>1, with
P=1+the sum of all earlier steps, D=1+the sum of all later steps,
B=the first selected step minus1, E=the second selected step minus1,
and C=1+the sum of the whole word. Before the selected pair every
gradient is u and each iterate is(1+the remaining prefix sum)u; its
proximal point is a nonnegative multiple of u, so the same two vertex
normal comparisons hold. After the selected pair every gradient is w
and each iterate is(1+the remaining suffix sum)w, with the same
nonnegative-multiple normal argument. All original intermediate samples
and all directed Q are thus retained. The initial point is P*u and the
terminal point is w, giving the same energy and(34). This paragraph
justifies applying the construction to the first pair as well as the
middle pair of the four-step word, without assuming either unselected
block is primitive.

## 12. The first endpoint also exceeds one

Suppose for a contradiction h_0<=1. The endpoint-sum consequence(28)
forces h_3>1. Since h_0-1<=0 and h_3-1<=r_cap(C), the same consequence
forces ell_cap(C)<=r_cap(C), or2C^3-15C^2-16C-3<=0. This polynomial
equals11/2 at17/2 and its derivative is positive for C>=7. Therefore
C<17/2. In the notation of Section11,

    1<P<=2, 2<D<3, B=h_1-1>0, E=h_2-1>0.

Rearranging the second-step cut and using monotonicity in P<=2 gives

    B<=P(C-P)/(C+P)<=2(C-2)/(C+2)<26/21.             (35)

The derivative with respect to P of P(C-P)/(C+P) has numerator
C^2-2CP-P^2>0 on C>=193/25,P<=2; the last expression increases in C.
Also(31) gives E<1552559/1081453<23/16, with the latter positive
difference32475/17303248. Thus both B and E are less than23/16.

We first show D<13/5. The suffix-pair inequality(18) becomes

    E <= (C-D)/(1+C(D-2)),                            (36)

since P+1+B=C-D-E and D>2. By the capacity identity and(35),

    E=C-P-D-1-B >= H(C)-D-1,
    H(C)=C(C-2)/(C+2).

Therefore feasibility requires

    F_sep(C,D)=H(C)-D-1-(C-D)/(1+C(D-2))<=0.           (37)

For C>=193/25 and13/5<=D<=3, this separation expression strictly
increases with C. Indeed H'=1-8/(C+2)^2>73/81, while the derivative
of the final rational term with respect to C is
(D-1)^2/[1+C(D-2)]^2<=25/169; their difference is positive.
For C_0=193/25 the expression is concave in D: its second derivative
is-2C_0(C_0-1)^2/[1+C_0(D-2)]^3<0. Its endpoint values are

    F_sep(C_0,13/5)=2269/66825>0,
    F_sep(C_0,3)=1166/662175>0.

Concavity puts its value above the chord joining these positive values.
It is therefore positive throughout this interval, contradicting(37).
This proves D<13/5.

Now B+E=C-P-D-1>53/25. Since B,E<23/16, multiplication of
(23/16-B)(23/16-E)>0 yields

    B*E>6279/6400, B>273/400.

For P<=2 and C>=193/25, (P+C)/P^2>=243/100; the expression decreases
with P>0 and increases with C. Consequently

    B*E*[1+B(P+C)/P^2]
       >(6279/6400)*(106339/40000)
       =13/5+2102581/256000000 >D,

contradicting the full triangle condition(34). Hence

    h_0>1.                                           (38)

Together with Section7, the first THREE steps exceed1. No reversal of
the schedule or symmetry of the certificate was used.

## 13. The last endpoint also exceeds one

Suppose now h_3<=1. Set alpha=h_0-1>0,b=h_1-1>0,c=h_2-1>0,
delta=h_3-1<=0. As in Section12, the endpoint-sum bound gives C<17/2.
The last-middle cut and the capacity identity imply

    c<=2(C-2)/(C+2),
    b=C-5-alpha-delta-c>=L(C,alpha),
    L(C,alpha)=C-7-alpha+8/(C+2).

Apply the adjacent triangle(34) to the first two steps, with prefix
capacity1 and suffix capacity1+h_2+h_3=C-2-alpha-b. It yields

    b<=J(C,alpha),
    J(C,alpha)=(C-2-alpha)/(1+alpha+alpha^2(C+1)).       (39)

We claim alpha<7/10. If7/10<=alpha<77/97<4/5, put
R=1+alpha+alpha^2(C+1) and N=C-2-alpha. On the rectangle
193/25<=C<=17/2,7/10<=alpha<=4/5 one has R>5 and

    J_C=[1+alpha+alpha^2(3+alpha)]/R^2<1/5,
    L_C=1-8/(C+2)^2>73/81.

Thus L-J increases with C. It also increases with alpha. Indeed
J_alpha=-(R+N R_alpha)/R^2, while L_alpha=-1. Here

    R<=197/25, N>=123/25, R_alpha>=1651/125,
    N R_alpha-R^2>=9028/3125>0.

Hence-J_alpha>1 and(L-J)_alpha>0. Finally the exact lower corner value is

    L(193/25,7/10)-J(193/25,7/10)=58247/22677975>0.

This contradicts(39), proving alpha<7/10.

For the middle adjacent pair now put P=1+h_0=2+alpha<27/10,
D=1+h_3<=2, and x=b+c=C-P-D-1. Then

    x>101/50, c<26/21,
    821/1050<b<783/560,
    (P+C)/P^2>=1042/729.                             (40)

The lower bound on b is x-c. For the upper bound use the cut
b<=P(C-P)/(C+P); this function increases in P<=27/10 and C>=193/25
on the current rectangle, and its value at(P,C)=(27/10,17/2) is783/560.
For its P derivative the numerator C^2-2CP-P^2 is positive at the
worst corner(C,P)=(193/25,27/10) and increases with C and decreases
with P; the C derivative is2P^2/(C+P)^2>0. The final inequality in(40)
uses monotonic decrease in P and increase in C of(P+C)/P^2.

Define the fixed cubic

    F_pair(b)=b*(101/50-b)*(1+(1042/729)b).

Since b<783/560<101/50, (40) implies

    b*c*[1+b(P+C)/P^2]>=F_pair(b).

The second derivative of F_pair is strictly decreasing in b and at
b=821/1050 equals-124646/42525<0. It is therefore concave on the
entire interval in(40), so its minimum is at an endpoint. The two
exact endpoint margins above2 are

    F_pair(821/1050)-2=211017368/4219543125>0,
    F_pair(783/560)-2=266455141/439040000>0.

Thus the middle-pair left side in(34) is greater than2>=D, a contradiction.
This proves h_3>1. All four original steps therefore satisfy

    1<h_0,h_3<174/97,
    1<h_1,h_2<2634012/1081453.                        (41)

This conclusion is derived; it was not assumed during the search-domain
reduction. It uses actual legal functions with all interpolation pairs.
All prefix differences |p_i-p_j| now exceed1. Since p_i<9 and4s<154/5,
the exact multiplier-energy identity(16) further gives

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij <112266/5<22500.              (42)

## 15. A backward extension strengthens the middle triangle

This is a new joint DRAFT calculation in the seventh launch's final round.
It is not a fact and consumes no verifier or numerical allowance. All four
steps retain their order. Write

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), a,b,c,d>0,
    C=5+a+b+c+d, P=2+a, D=2+d, U=C-P,
    H=U-b=1+c+D, eta=b/H,
    kappa(a)=(1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,
    kappa_plus=max(kappa(a),0),
    R=P^2-kappa_plus, J=P^2-kappa_plus*(1+eta).

The preceding full-domain derivation gives a<1 and the b-cut gives
eta<=P/C, hence a*eta<1 (indeed a*P/C<3/7). Then a stronger necessary
inequality than the middle adjacent triangle is

    b*c*[R+b(P+C)] <= D*J.                           (43)

For a<=sqrt(2)-1 this is exactly(34). For a>sqrt(2)-1 the positive
term subtracted from the old triangle slack is strictly positive.
No relation between c and b, or saturation assumption, is imposed.

Here is the entire new interpolation check. The case D<=b*c is already
excluded by the explicit capped function in Section11. Thus D>b*c. Set

    K=b^2*c+U*(D-b*c)=U*D-b*c*H,
    r=b^2*c/K,
    u=(1,0,0), w=(r,sqrt(r*(1-r)),0),
    v=-(b*u+D*w)/(1+c).

We have 0<r<1, <u,w>=||w||^2=r, and 0 lies in conv{u,v,w}.
These are exactly the middle triangle vectors, but its initial prefix
will now change. Choose t in[-eta,1] and put

    q=(1+(a-1)*t)/a,
    z=t*u+sqrt(q-t^2)*(0,0,1).

The radicand is (1-t)(1+a*t)/a>=0 because a*eta<1. Define the actual
positions and gradients by

    x=(u+(1+a)*z, u, -b*u, D*w, w),
    g=(z,u,v,w,w),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.

The four GD equalities hold. Add the optimum jet(0,0,0). Each proximal
point y_i=x_i-g_i must support conv{z,u,v,w} at g_i. This suffices for
all ordered Q because Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>.

At i=0, y_0=u+a*z. Since t+a*q=1+a*t and every old vertex y in
{0,u,v,w} has <u,y><=1,

    <y-z,y_0>=(1+a*t)(<u,y>-1)<=0.

At i=1 and i=4 the proximal point is zero. At i=3 it equals(1+d)*w.
Its pairing with z-w is (1+d)*r*(t-1)<=0; pairings with u-w and v-w
are respectively zero and negative, as explicitly computed in Section11.

At i=2 let n=-b*u-v. The two old vertex comparisons are

    <w-v,n>=0, <u-v,n>=-b*(1-r)*c/(1+c)<0.

The remaining comparison with z is exact. Put A_num=D*r-b*c. Then

    <u,n>=A_num/(1+c),
    <v,n>=r*(D-b*c)/(1+c),
    A_num=-b*c*H*(D-b*c)/K<0,
    r*(D-b*c)=-eta*A_num.

The fresh coordinate of z is orthogonal to n. Consequently

    <z-v,n>=(t+eta)*A_num/(1+c)<=0.

The optimum comparisons follow as 0 is already a convex combination
of u,v,w. This checks every original ordered pair, including those
involving the modified initial gradient. Fact1252b8823bb5a290 realizes
the complete array by one global convex1-smooth function with minimizer0.
No gradient Gram from any other schedule has been reused.

The energy is ||x_0||^2-C^2*r by the directly derived identity(3), and

    ||x_0||^2
      =1+2(1+a)t+(1+a)^2*q
      =P^2+kappa(a)*(t-1).

Choose t=1 if kappa(a)<=0 and t=-eta otherwise. The initial squared
norm is exactly J. Nonnegative energy therefore requires

    C^2*b^2*c <= J*K.

Using K=U*D-b*c*H, C-P=U, and J*H=P^2*H-kappa_plus*U gives

    C^2*b^2*c-J*K
      =U*{b*c*[P^2-kappa_plus+b(P+C)]-D*J},

which proves(43). Compared to the original triangle's forbidden-side
expression, the new left-minus-right expression increases by

    kappa_plus*[D*(1+eta)-b*c]
      =kappa_plus*K/H.

This is strictly positive whenever a>sqrt(2)-1. Thus(43) is a genuine
strengthening throughout that region, rather than a relabeling of the
old test. This last algebraic comparison is a strength comparison only;
it does not by itself prove there exists a word passing every old test
and failing the new one.

For rational elimination on the a>sqrt(2)-1 branch, use

    R=a+3+1/a,
    J=R-kappa(a)*b/(C-2-a-b),
    c <= J*(C-3-a-b)/(J+b*[R+b(C+2+a)]),              (44)

where c+d=C-5-a-b and D=2+d. All denominators are positive: H>0,
J=||x_0||^2>0, b>0, R>0. The strict positivity of J follows also
because its fresh coordinate is nonzero at t=-eta. Equations(43),(44)
retain the independent variables C,a,b,d (with c recovered by the sum).

## 16. A second extension retains both initial gradients and their normals

Continue the same joint draft, with h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d) and the same
positive a,b,c,d. This section constructs a different legal witness,
with its last triangle at steps2 and3. Its symbols are local to this
section and do not change the definitions in Section15.

The old adjacent triangle at the middle pair implies b*c<2+d, and the
last adjacent triangle implies c*d<1. Set

    U_tail=c+d+2,
    K_tail=c^2*d+U_tail*(1-c*d),
    r_tail=c^2*d/K_tail,
    eta_tail=c/(d+2),
    A_pre=1+a+b, H_pre=A_pre+1=2+a+b,
    Z_tail=(c+r_tail)/(1+d),
    q_late=(1+(1-b)*eta_tail)/b,
    q_early=[eta_tail+(A_pre*eta_tail-1)*Z_tail]/A_pre,
    q_pre=max(q_late,q_early),
    J_pre=1-2H_pre*eta_tail+H_pre^2*q_pre.

Every denominator is positive. We have 0<r_tail<1, b*eta_tail<1, and

    q_late-eta_tail^2
      =(1+eta_tail)(1-b*eta_tail)/b>0.

The resulting new necessary inequality is

    C^2*c^2*d <= J_pre*K_tail.                       (45)

The old last-pair triangle is retained too. Equivalently the right
side can be replaced by min(J_pre,(3+a+b)^2)*K_tail. This use of a
minimum means choosing the stronger of two independently legal witnesses.
It imposes no equality between formal gradients in the full certificate.

For the complete construction take

    u=(1,0,0),
    w=(r_tail,sqrt(r_tail*(1-r_tail)),0),
    v=-(c*u+w)/(1+d),
    z=-eta_tail*u+sqrt(q_pre-eta_tail^2)*(0,0,1).

As before 0 lies in conv{u,v,w}. Define all five original samples by

    x=(u+H_pre*z, u+(1+b)*z, u, -c*u, w),
    g=(z,z,u,v,w),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,

and add the zero optimum jet. Each GD step has exactly its original
length. We check the complete normal list for conv{z,u,v,w}.

At indices2 and4 the normal is zero. At index3 it is n=-c*u-v.
The triangle calculation gives

    <w-v,n>=0,
    <u-v,n>=-c*(1-r_tail)*d/(1+d)<0,
    <z-v,n>=0.

For the last equality the Section15 calculation uses first-pair
shift c, second-pair shift d, suffix capacity1, and t=-eta_tail.
It is independent of q_pre because the fresh coordinate is orthogonal
to n. Explicitly, with B_num=r_tail-c*d<0,

    <u,n>=B_num/(1+d),
    <v,n>=r_tail*(1-c*d)/(1+d),
    r_tail*(1-c*d)=-eta_tail*B_num.

At index1 the normal is u+b*z. For an old vertex y in{0,u,v,w},

    <y-z,u+b*z>
      =(1-b*eta_tail)<u,y>+eta_tail-b*q_pre.

The coefficient of <u,y> is positive and its maximum over the old
vertices is at u. The choice q_pre>=q_late makes that maximum<=0.

At index0 the normal is u+A_pre*z. Its comparison with y is

    (1-A_pre*eta_tail)<u,y>+eta_tail-A_pre*q_pre.

The old values <u,y> lie between -Z_tail (at v) and1 (at u).
The v comparison is nonpositive by q_pre>=q_early. At u, even the
smaller q_late gives the exact value

    (1+eta_tail)*(1-A_pre/b)<0,

since A_pre>b. These two endpoints control the entire interval,
including0 andw, for either sign of1-A_pre*eta_tail. The pairs
between indices0 and1 have the same gradient and hence Q=0. This
exhausts all original directed and optimum interpolation inequalities.
